# Loopora Contracts

These contracts define what must stay true while implementation stays free to shrink.

## Test Asset Governance

Contract tests prove stable claims once; use small smoke checks only across layers.

| Stable claim | Primary proof | Smoke | Avoid |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| v3 Agent envelope shape | v3 helper + summary/projection tests | CLI JSON actionability | Duplicated envelope helpers, `raw.legacy` as contract |
| Agent Native work panel, proof/lifecycle split, submit repair | Projection/submit-repair tests | Plain command smoke | Full prose locking, re-derived CLI proof semantics |
| Adapter managed entries and native guidance | Section/semantic-marker tests | Init/check smoke | Long entry snippets, adapter copy as proof |
| Web projection | Projection/file-preview/event/bundle/orchestration/network tests | Minimal journey smoke | Raw records or DOM/CSS internals |
| Alignment readiness and source context | Readiness/session/source-context tests | Web/API stream smoke | Full transcript, exact assistant prose, executor internals |
| Runtime verdict and evidence coverage | Verdict/evidence coverage tests | CLI/Web run-detail smoke | Process success, todo state, or host trace as proof |
| Legacy Agent summary placement | One v3-helper diagnostic placement test | Real-probe notes | New `raw.legacy` field contracts |
| Real provider and Agent probes | Manual workflow/docs contract proves opt-in release evidence and keeps `.loopora/real-probes/` phase reports downloadable from failed or skipped workflow runs | GitHub workflow/docs tests | Release probe run notes | Live credentials, host sessions, or provider state as ordinary PR/default-fast gates |

Open-source security support scope follows the source-only alpha boundary: the current development line and maintainer-supported release tags are in scope, unmarked snapshots/forks/dirty checkouts should reproduce against those supported lines, and older local state migration remains design-explicit rather than implied by package version.

Release readiness is a maintainer-owned open-source collaboration boundary: supported tag or distribution decisions tie together the candidate version/tag, supported status, default-fast and focused evidence, browser journeys for touched Web behavior, the default `Real Probe` release suite, package-build contents/no-license checks, `CHANGELOG.md` release notes, skipped probes, residual risks, and generated artifact cleanup. It must not become an ordinary pull request gate.

Public doctor reports use schema v2 when they include coarse package, source revision and dirty/clean state when available, project-directory status, Python, OS, implementation, machine-architecture identity, and redacted Web readiness blockers for issue reproduction, but must not include local paths, host/port values, executable commands, tokens, or full local task examples. The legacy scalar Web start blocker remains the bind/auth blocker for compatibility; combined readiness blockers carry App-state plus bind/auth causes so public reports do not hide a required App reset behind a port or auth problem.
When App-state and Web bind/auth/port blockers coexist, doctor may still report the Web blocker and suggested recovery, but Web start/port/auth action items remain blocked until App readiness and plain/public summaries must not present those commands as immediately executable.
Public doctor summaries expose redacted `next_action_kinds` alongside compatibility `next_actions`, `next_action_summaries`, and `readiness_axes`, so issue triage and automation can read the same action-kind field shape plus coarse project-directory, same-Agent entry, App-state, Web-start, and first-task handoff readiness without exposing commands or local paths.

Contributor PR evidence treats the default-fast gate as both verification and generated-artifact cleanup.
Contributors should paste the final stage-labeled `--pr-evidence` Markdown block as the main local evidence for decision scope, focused guide IDs, unmatched/ignored changed-file lines, explicit `--focused-ran` coverage, skipped boundary checks, final default-fast result, package-build cleanup state, and extra journey/review/probe notes when relevant.
Focused-check pass output points to final PR evidence only after selected guide IDs cover the recommended boundary; partial explicit focused passes carry already-passed IDs into the remaining guide command.

## Stable Boundary Map

Core user-path anchors stay visible before detailed subsystem rows: The compiler turns task judgment into a reviewable and runnable Loop. Web dialogue, Agent candidate plans, YAML import/export, preview, and run creation all use the same bundle normalization and semantic lint. The default user model is linear and explainable: `Builder -> optional Inspector/Guide -> GateKeeper`.

Core Agent Native path anchors stay visible before detailed subsystem rows: Agent Native lets the current Coding Agent remain the execution subject while Loopora owns planning/review, run-stage recovery, host-native dispatch through role dispatch, evidence handoffs, submit acceptance, and task-proof/lifecycle separation. `/loopora-plan` creates, revises, repairs, or tightens reviewed Loop previews; `/loopora-run` starts, resumes, replays, or continues evidence under that reviewed Loop.

RunEngine step evidence recording is an atomic event-chain command: `EvidenceSubmitted` is followed by causally linked `EvidenceAccepted`, optional `EvidenceLinkedToTarget`, and `CoverageRecomputed`. The evidence ledger still projects accepted evidence entries as the stable read model, while audit and coverage can prove the submitted evidence was linked to targets before coverage was recomputed.

RunEngine verdict issuing is atomic: `VerdictRequested` precedes `VerdictIssued`, then `VerdictAllowedClosure` or `VerdictBlockedClosure`. Residual risk adds `ResidualRiskAccepted`; blocked gaps add `NextGapSelected`. Task verdict projections still derive from `VerdictIssued`; closure audit facts do not replace successful `RunClosed` causation.

RunEngine step claiming records `StepPlanned` before `StepClaimed`, followed by the causally linked `StepInstructionIssued` that freezes the executable StepInstruction. Current-step and surface projections still use `StepInstructionIssued`; `StepPlanned` and `StepClaimed` are audit facts around planning and runner ownership.

RunEngine step submission records `StrategyAdvanced` after causally linked `StepCommitted`; it mirrors the latest committed step id/iteration/status as an audit fact, while the next executable step still comes from replay plus the current strategy definition.

Agent Native managed authoring and runtime projections are schema guards as well as prompt guidance.
Agent Native `/loopora-plan` managed authoring skeleton and Web alignment bundle guidance are schema guards as well as prompt guidance.
They must keep host-native alignment and explicit confirmation before candidate submission, default to Builder -> Inspector -> GateKeeper, preserve Success Surface bullets, Role Notes `## <Role Name> Notes` subheadings, non-GateKeeper `on_pass: "continue"`, structured `inputs.evidence_query`, GateKeeper `on_pass: "finish_run"`, and user-display-language names/prose while keeping YAML keys and required headings stable.
Agent Native `/loopora-run` managed recovery guidance and terminal `task_next_action` projection must surface `retry_lifecycle_failure`, `linked_run_lifecycle_failure`, and `recording_blocked_reason` as lifecycle retry state rather than evidence continuation.

Public Web alignment-session creation requires a non-empty task message so API clients cannot bypass the first transcript's task anchor. A task goal is sufficient to start the Web conversation.
Loopora's clarifying and working-agreement stages must collect missing fit, success, fake-done, evidence, tradeoff, residual-risk, and governance judgment before a runnable candidate can become READY.
Users with an existing review may include known judgment in the first task message. That message is also recorded as an `alignment_user_message` event so transcript, event stream, and audit views share the same user evidence.

Web conversation checks the selected executor before initial generation and generation retry. Definitive missing commands, invalid command configuration, or unavailable runtime factories block submission and open Agent settings before a session is created; embedded/test executors remain host-managed readiness. This read-only check exposes no resolved executable path and proves command resolution only, not authentication or model health. Runtime failures still preserve the task through alignment generation recovery.

Public Web alignment-session seed recovery keeps Plan File persistence ahead of session usability. Seeded alignment-session creation writes the seed Plan File before creating the session record; seed write failures return stable alignment-session creation errors plus source-review/retry/support recovery instead of leaving a half-created session.
Browser recovery keeps source-review and retry-create actions executable from the alignment page. Revision-session seed write failures mark the created revision session failed and report a stable revision-session creation error with recovery instead of leaving an idle session with no usable seed Plan File.

Web alignment event console restore semantics are event contracts, not layout contracts. The Web live event console projects stable alignment-event semantics such as task input, source context, session lifecycle, validation, repair, READY, import, and run-start state instead of exposing only raw event type names; when restoring an existing session, it loads historical event pages to the current tail before subscribing to live events so terminal READY, failure, import, or run-start events do not disappear behind the first page; exact UI copy, JSON field order, and console layout remain non-contracts.

Selected source-file context may carry redacted file content into the working agreement, but unreadable source files project a stable source-unreadable meaning instead of server-local paths or low-level exception text. Model-visible selected source context omits absolute local source paths while preserving relative artifact refs and selected content. Broken historical workdir-context candidates are skipped during source discovery, while explicit selections that become unavailable report stable selected-context-unavailable meaning. Service-level idle sessions may still exist for internal recovery or deferred-start compatibility.

Reviewed task judgment must remain the source of domain routing and repair handoffs. Domain-specific long-chain workflow routing and secondary-object precedence live in `domain-workflow-contracts.md`; the invariant here is that reviewed task judgment must project into the working agreement, spec, role prompts, step inputs, evidence buckets, governance responsibilities, and GateKeeper fan-in rather than merely adding roles.

Reviewed task grounding and repair handoffs remain evidence-bound. Workdir grounding checks reject unsupported observed-stack claims, but fake-done or fail-closed phrases such as frontend-only evidence are risk descriptions rather than observed stack facts. Repair Builder steps after a review-shaped Guide must read both the Guide handoff and the review handoffs that shaped it, so repair execution does not replace original evidence with a detached summary.

When a not-fit clarifying turn offers `skip_loop` and the user selects it, the alignment session is skipped without writing a bundle, starting a run, or re-asking the same Loop-fit question.

Alignment decision choices shown to users are bounded, complete, and carry exactly one recommended path. Incomplete or ambiguous model-provided sets, including sets with zero or multiple recommended choices, fall back to the deterministic choice catalog for the current clarifying, agreement, or not-fit stage.

Alignment transcript persistence remains complete for audit and user-visible history, while the model-visible prompt uses a bounded projection that retains the first user task anchor and the newest decision branch. The projection reports omitted entries and content truncation explicitly; resolved judgments must remain available through the working agreement instead of depending on unlimited ambient transcript replay.

Alignment prompt guidance is stage-aware: clarifying and agreement-confirmation turns retain conversation, fit, risk, and stage-control guidance without loading the full bundle contract; confirmed, compiling, ready-review, and repair turns retain the complete bundle contract and compilation examples. Improvement-only guidance is loaded only when the working agreement is an improvement flow, so early dialogue does not pay for unrelated compiler context.

Alignment Workdir Snapshot is model-visible context: unreadable workdir inspection projects a stable workdir-unreadable message instead of OS exception text or local permission/path details.

Alignment bundle file sync validation and preview: preview/reload/save failures are validation states. Missing files, unreadable bundle content, invalid UTF-8/YAML, semantic validation failures, and normalized bundle save failures update or project stable validation state with repairable messages rather than OS exception text, permission details, or server-local paths.
Preview missing-file responses use current missing-file validation instead of stale session validation. Web preview, sync, and import failures also project review/sync/retry/support actions with browser-executable sync, retry-preview, and retry-import controls so the READY repair path does not collapse into a passive validation error.

Alignment candidate YAML write safety: candidate bundle YAML writes use atomic replacement. A failed validation/sync/import normalization save preserves the previous candidate file and clears temporary output instead of making the repairable Plan File itself untrustworthy.

Alignment current-bundle prompt context: current bundle prompt context uses the stable current-bundle-unreadable meaning instead of letting path-probe or read errors enter model-visible context.

Alignment READY import failure split: READY bundle import separates import success from Run dispatch failure. Failed service import after validation restores the original candidate YAML instead of committing a normalized rewrite as a side effect, keeps the session repairable at READY, and projects review/sync/retry/support recovery through the Web import API instead of a bare error. When the Bundle and Run have been created but the background worker cannot start, the session records the linked failed Run and API clients receive a Run-detail redirect with stable action feedback rather than an import-failed response.

Alignment run-start failure event recovery: the alignment run-start failure event carries retry-start recovery, next-action kinds, and readiness projections for event-list and SSE clients instead of leaving them to infer recovery from a raw error string.

Damaged Agent context cards are recovery states rather than raw file diagnostics: service run-start, CLI run-start, and context-choice surfaces expose stable repair-context-card errors without the local binding path, parser details, or OS exception text.

Agent candidate plan-file recovery remains task-message gated: `/loopora-plan --bundle-file` first reports `plan_message_required` when `--message` is missing.
After task context exists, unresolvable, unreadable, directory-shaped, unavailable, or locally unsavable Agent candidate plan files are repair states rather than raw file diagnostics.
Candidate repair reports `repair_candidate_plan_file`, preserves the supplied candidate path as the repair anchor, preserves the task message for traceability, marks any created alignment session failed when the submitted candidate cannot be saved into session artifacts, and omits OS exception text or unrelated local paths from plain and JSON output. Agent candidate intake/recovery code is split across the `service_agent_bundle_candidate_*` modules while `service_agent_bundle_candidates.py` remains the compatibility import surface.

Agent context-card save failures after `/loopora-plan` creates or continues a preview are repair states rather than anonymous command errors: the preview session remains addressable, JSON reports `loop_recovery=repair_agent_context_card`, and repair guidance targets project `.loopora` Agent-state write access before `/loopora-run` can rely on same-Agent context.

Agent context-card save failures after `/loopora-run` creates or resumes a Run are warnings rather than task or lifecycle failures: the Run remains usable through explicit run-id/submit commands, result summaries include `context_binding_error` and a context repair action, and low-level OS errors or binding file paths are not exposed in the failed-save projection.

Agent context-card save failures while binding an explicit recoverable context choice happen before Run start and remain `repair_context_card` recovery states: CLI JSON/plain recovery reports the stable context-card save error and check command without selecting a different context, starting a Run, or exposing low-level OS/path diagnostics.

Agent Native submit selected result-file read failures are detected before App-state recovery and remain submit-repair states: missing, directory-shaped, unreadable, non-UTF-8, invalid JSON, or non-object files return `agent_submit_repair` guidance for the selected file instead of App-state reset recovery or a generic command error, while available service context may still enrich active-step repair details.

Strategy Source and role prompt file CLI inputs are Loopora validation states rather than Typer usage failures or raw IO diagnostics: missing, directory-shaped, unreadable, or non-UTF-8 `--strategy-file` / `--workflow-file`, `--prompt-file`, and prompt validation path inputs produce stable strategy/prompt-file errors.
Flow asset `--strategy-file` errors are owned by `cli_orchestration_strategy_file_recovery.py` and return repair/choose/preset next actions before App-state or saved-Flow lookup, so a broken selected file does not force unrelated local database recovery.
Role asset `--prompt-file` errors are owned by `cli_role_prompt_file_recovery.py` and return repair/choose/validate next actions without echoing the selected local prompt path or offering a retry command that points back to an unusable file.
Those inputs expand home-directory paths inside the Strategy Source boundary before IO, do not create or update Loops, orchestrations, or role definitions, and omit OS exception text or local paths from user-facing output.
Prompt commands are expert Strategy Source asset surfaces: help output keeps them behind reviewed Loop planning and routes first-use planning back through `loopora start`, `loopora fit`, Fit Guide/Web choices, or same-Agent setup/readiness before `/loopora-plan`; missing template/validate inputs and invalid prompt-file inputs return recovery payloads rather than Typer usage or anonymous JSON errors, list JSON failures stay structured, and template rendering with a selected ref remains a plain Markdown artifact stream.
These commands do not replace `loopora fit`, `/loopora-plan`, Web review, or expert spec artifact repair, and prompt validation success does not prove a Loop is READY or safe to run.

Alignment READY validation treats confirmed judgment traceability as a hard compiler boundary. A generated bundle with non-info control-summary diagnostics for missing traceability is not READY: project-local governance markers must be mapped through Builder reading, Inspector / Custom verification, and finishing GateKeeper Weak / Unproven / Blocking or fail-closed treatment instead of remaining only in agreement text, summary prose, or partial role prompts.

First-use diagnostics next actions are dependency-ordered: missing Agent entries lead with install, then a read-only `loopora doctor --workdir <project>` readiness confirmation before returning to the Agent, Agent refresh, and `/loopora-plan`; when the diagnostic used a non-default Web host or port, that readiness confirmation preserves the same Web target so copyable recovery does not drift back to the default service, and generated Web start commands preserve `--workdir <project>` so Web opens against the inspected target.

The `loopora fit` guide is also a first-use router: completed strong-fit reviews present Fit Guide/Web choices first outside Agent sessions, route same-Agent users through the uniquely detected current Codex/Claude Code/OpenCode host when available, keep explicit adapter choice as the unavailable/ambiguous fallback before install/readiness and `/loopora-plan`, and keep support as a read-only local route action outside review/setup/direct-path gates.
Fit guidance limits route choice to reviewed task and current readiness evidence; premature route expansion must not outrun fit, target-project, current-host, setup, or READY gates.
It keeps review-complete `/loopora-plan` message readiness separate from target-project setup executability, keeps targetless plain route details hidden until a target-project `loopora fit --workdir "$PWD"` rerun while JSON retains blocked route actions, and leaves `/loopora-run` behind READY review instead of making the task Agent-only or setup-first.
The `loopora start` wrapper preserves that separation when it embeds fit guidance: a complete review with no usable target is blocked by target-project state, not by missing review inputs, and targetless choose-workdir next actions preserve the current CLI entry/language plus supplied review inputs while rerunning the route guide from the target project with `--workdir "$PWD"` instead of placing `<project-dir>` in next actions; targetless support remains a separate route-only exit.
Plain `loopora start` keeps the copyable first `/loopora-plan` message and direct-path fallback ahead of setup and route detail, so the reviewed task handoff and no-Loopora decision stay visible before install, Web, doctor, or run choices.
Incomplete-review plain output hides route command previews while JSON keeps blocked route actions; completed-review route previews omit `check_fit_first`; embedded fit guidance route/kind summaries mirror the top-level start route; complete-review plain output stops repeating unanswered setup questions.
Incomplete-review JSON keeps the decision-only `continue_if_strong_fit` action blocked until review inputs are complete; plain first-use next actions use readable labels that separate ready-now route choices from install-dependent doctor confirmation, and JSON publishes ready-now/dependent readiness fields beside stable next/route action kinds.
Fit JSON, start JSON, and the Web Fit Guide context expose top-level task-review supplied/status fields, fit/setup allowance, next/route/direct-path action-kind lists, direct-path readiness/blocker projections, and workdir readiness state so clients do not parse prose before choosing the next first-use action.
Targetless Fit Guide contexts publish `workdir_state.status=required`; request-scoped Web Fit Guide pages mirror the explicit request workdir into machine-readable page state before route/setup readiness is projected, and browser-generated completion commands or handoffs use that page state when no link/URL/current Web target workdir is more specific. Global Web target changes refresh visible Fit Guide completion/direct-path commands so manually copied command text does not keep an old target.
Explicit unusable or direct-path target projects in `loopora start` and `loopora fit` keep route/setup actions blocked while surfacing an immediate read-only support/report action; targetless direct-path decisions still expose route-only support, and targetless cold-start/incomplete/complete reviews expose the same route-only support while setup/report route commands still ask users to choose a target project first.
Embedded fit guidance Web preflight summary and local readiness/support commands mirror the top-level start route so JSON clients do not see a blocked Web command paired with stale `not_checked` preflight status or Web-target drift after a suggested-port recovery.

When launched through a source-checkout `uv run loopora` or `uv run python -m loopora` entry, its copyable review-completion and setup commands preserve that same entry before install, while adapter alternatives such as codex/claude/opencode are exposed as choices or notes instead of inline shell comments inside command fields.

Browser-session fit-review drafts may be shown without consuming them. Matching complete or partial drafts can continue into Web conversation, which carries known task judgment and clarifies missing items; same-Agent setup commands still require a complete review. Wrong-project drafts stay separate and surface a switch-to-source action, while an explicit direct-path decision blocks Loopora creation for the matching target until the decision is cleared or reviewed again.

The root CLI help is the shell-level first-use router: it leads with read-only `loopora start`, keeps `loopora fit` before route choice when fit is uncertain, presents Web continuation before same-Agent setup for users outside an Agent session, and routes current-host setup through explicit `loopora init current` detection with named-adapter fallback.
Before those real-task routes, root help exposes `loopora demo --open` as an optional zero-configuration product evaluation path. Demo runs the normal Core compiler, runner, evidence, verdict, and Web projection against bundled task contracts and a deterministic simulated executor; it does not claim Agent/provider readiness or real-task proof.
Demo exposes two explicit paths under one isolated lifecycle: a completed evidence-sample project for inspecting proof and verdict semantics, and a separate initially empty composition playground for trying Fit and one-sentence Web creation without inheriting sample artifacts or returning-user cues. The persistent Web disclosure links both paths, and Demo Compose/Fit navigation targets the playground instead of the evidence project.
Demo binds loopback only, selects an available local port, uses a temporary App home and projects instead of normal user state, opens the completed Run when requested, marks simulated evidence across the Web shell, and deletes its App, both projects, and all artifacts when the foreground process exits. A process-wide workdir scope resolves candidate and symlink targets before project reads or writes, accepts only the temporary Demo root and its descendants, and fails closed before Loop, Alignment, Run, or same-Agent project state can target anything outside that root.
Demo Web keeps the active temporary target visible but does not offer all-project or arbitrary-path switching; composition directory controls remain fixed to the playground even when a request supplies another path. Normal `serve`, App home, records, settings, and recent targets remain outside this lifecycle.
Leaving Demo for a real task is an explicit environment handoff, not a target switch inside the simulated service. CLI and Web expose a copyable normal `loopora start --workdir "$PWD"` command, preserving source-checkout entry and selected language while deliberately omitting Demo's temporary `LOOPORA_HOME`; the user stops Demo and runs it from the real target project.
Demo `--language` selects one bundled task language for terminal lifecycle text and deterministic Core prose, including generated checks, coverage labels/reasons, simulated role output and handoffs, and the task verdict. Stable role, status, schema, target, check, and evidence identifiers stay language-neutral; Web chrome continues to follow request locale rather than the CLI option.
It stays scan-friendly by showing the target-project `loopora start --workdir "$PWD"` and task-shaped fit entry first, then delegating the full decision record, route catalog, and recovery diagnostics to explicit `--details`. Root help keeps the outside-Agent versus current-host distinction and the READY follow-up rule without reproducing the full route tutorial or placeholder command catalog before the command table.
The default root command table is ordered as `start`, `fit`, `demo`, `serve`, `init`, `doctor`, then `support`; the contributor local-checks entry stays visible in help prose, while advanced/internal commands stay reachable through their own help and hidden from the default first-use table.
Its support entry points users to `loopora support --workdir "$PWD"` so readiness-related public diagnostics start target-project ready instead of preview-only, and its root help points Chinese readers to `start`, `fit`, and `support` with `--language zh` while keeping JSON action kinds language-neutral.
The start and fit default plain outputs use progressive disclosure: they show the current review state, one recommended executable next action, at most one terminal alternative, and the active setup/create/run gate. They do not print incomplete message drafts, every route, or recovery catalogs by default.
`--details` preserves the complete human-readable fit record, route readiness, alternatives, and recovery diagnostics, while `--json` preserves the full structured contract unchanged.
Detailed route-guide option wording remains available there; route/support commands preserve target workdir and language selection.
Targetless output keeps the target-project rerun, target-aware incomplete review prefers Web continuation when available, completed review keeps Web versus current-host same-Agent choice visible, and READY review still gates run.
Generated Start/Fit Web actions use `serve --open` as one command. A new service stays foreground with visible Ctrl-C ownership; a responding same-App-home service at the requested origin is reused and the opener exits without bind/build/start. Plain `serve`, foreign App homes, non-Loopora services, and auth mismatches keep port-conflict behavior. Both paths preserve target/Fit context in a validated relative path; browser failure does not weaken auth, App-state, bind, or route gates.

The `loopora fit` help, `loopora init` group and adapter init subcommand help, root doctor help, grouped diagnose doctor help, `loopora serve --workdir <project>`, and shell recovery for Agent slash commands are first-use routers rather than only adapter/command hints.
They keep scan-friendly dependency ordering from `loopora fit` before setup when fit is uncertain, install-dependent doctor confirmation before `/loopora-plan` where same-Agent readiness gates apply, READY preview review before same-Agent `/loopora-run` while Fit Guide/Web choices continue in Web.
They also preserve Web target consistency for readiness/start guidance, Fit Guide/Web choice, review, and observation as the peer path for users outside those same-Agent gates, and stable target-directory recovery before Web starts.
Top-level internal `loopora agent` help routes users back through the target-project start guide before Web/init/doctor commands instead of exposing a direct Web serve command.
First-task guidance stays on the same completed-fit-review-first handoff policy as adapter install.

Manual Web and same-Agent Tools target edits, including typed edits before blur, must stay reflected in the current URL, visible context, peer navigation, Fit Guide first-use route CTAs, manual compose/import form submissions, standalone Plan File import/export form submissions, reusable asset catalog entry points, reusable asset editor form/API submissions and auxiliary navigation, and nested return-to links for Roles/Flows catalog round trips.
Browser-side target synchronization also updates page-local current-workdir state and broadcasts a workdir-change event used by route bridges and unbound Web conversation drafts, so Fit Guide handoff mismatch checks do not keep comparing against the server-rendered initial target after an in-page edit. Existing link, redirect, or nested `return_to` workdir contexts are preserved only when they are absolute target paths; relative workdir contexts are removed or replaced by the current safe absolute target.
Same-Agent Setup initializes its target project from the page's request-scoped workdir context before falling back to an old browser preference, while an explicit URL workdir remains the most specific source.
Explicit Web target URLs take precedence over stale browser Manual drafts.
Explicit same-Agent Tools target selections from trusted UI sources such as Fit Guide handoff, recent targets, the service directory, or native folder selection immediately update visible context, navigation, URL, and browser preference.
Free-form same-Agent Tools target edits replace stale visible/navigation context with the pending input, while browser target preferences persist only after the service returns a resolved target identity.
Cleared Web conversation/manual Web/same-Agent Tools targets remove URL/form/peer-link workdir context.
Run detail and fullscreen Console navigation preserve the current Web target-project context when opening the popout and returning to the run, so deep inspection does not silently drop the selected project.
Home existing-work rows/cards including clickable card bodies, Loop/Run/Plan detail actions including Plan File replacement and linked Role/Flow edit exits, and Plan File list cards/actions including clickable card bodies preserve that same target-project context when reopening loops, runs, plan files, and run-start/result-recording/revision/delete flows; successful, recoverable, and delete fallback redirects keep the same context, so moving from overview back into work or continuing from a detail page cannot silently switch projects.
Support target-project selection uses the same Web target context boundary: scoped Support, non-self global navigation, global workdir changes, recovery actions, and last-resort Web error pages keep safe local `return_to` handoffs without leaking token-bearing query keys; successful target selection keeps the selected absolute target for public evidence, global target changes update the standalone Support public-bundle controls as report-only until refreshed, and blank, relative, or invalid submissions clear stale target context instead of preserving an old project.
Global Web shell responsiveness is a first-use access boundary: on narrow viewports the primary navigation remains reachable without compressing labels into unreadable columns, the selected target-project context remains visible as a readable path, mobile touch controls keep usable target size, and support target actions stack instead of clipping target selection controls.
Global Web navigation mirrors the first-use decision order: existing work stays first, Fit Guide comes before creation and same-Agent setup, advanced reusable assets come after those start routes, and Support remains a stable reporting endpoint.
Global Web CSS layers are part of that same access boundary: templates load theme/base/layout/components/pages/legacy/app styles directly through cache-busted static asset URLs, so fixing a layer does not rely on browser refreshes of unversioned CSS imports.
Global Web brand assets use the same cache-busted template boundary as CSS: favicon, navigation lockup, auth lockup, and empty-state logos render through versioned logo asset URLs, and dark-mode logo swaps stay in template-owned image elements rather than CSS `content: url(...)` imports.

Root help points users to Start/Fit `--details` for the complete Fit Guide/Web, current-host same-Agent, import/manual expert, and existing-work choice model; init group help, adapter init/check subcommand help, serve help, and serve startup summaries keep the relevant detailed choices visible rather than leaving users with only a URL.
Targetless init group help marks setup commands preview-only and points to `loopora init --workdir "$PWD"` for target-project adapter choices before copying install/doctor/Web commands, while still exposing placeholder-safe support guidance. Doctor help also keeps the current-host Agent entry checkpoint, automation JSON, and redacted public issue reports separate.

Local JSON next-action commands for fit, install, safe App reset preview, readiness confirmation, Web start, disposable temporary Web preview with `LOOPORA_HOME="$(mktemp -d)" <current Loopora CLI entry> serve ...`, CLI help/recovery commands, and shell slash-command recovery preserve configured `LOOPORA_HOME` where applicable, preserve the current CLI entry such as source-checkout `uv --directory <Loopora checkout> run loopora`, avoid duplicated command prefixes and inline shell comments when help is rendered through nested command paths, and shell-quote local paths, so projects or App homes with spaces remain directly copyable.

Ready Agent entries still surface the host-visibility refresh hint before `/loopora-plan`, because static managed files do not prove the host Agent has reloaded its slash entries. Doctor and adapter check structured recovery use the same `return_to_agent` action kind for that user step, while older `return_to_ready_agent` inputs remain a rendering compatibility alias.

When local App/Web state blocks Web review, start/fit Web route actions expose safe App reset preview plus disposable temporary Web preview recoveries before Web start, readiness confirmation, and `/loopora-plan`; destructive reset still requires an explicit later `--yes`, and temporary preview is omitted when port/bind preflight already blocks the Web target.

Fit Guide Web start handoff gate: every visible Fit Guide continuation into Web, creation choice, same-Agent setup, or expert creation shares one route gate; an explicit direct-path decision applies to every Loopora creation start on the creation-choice page for the matching target. Partial fit drafts may continue into Web clarification, while wrong-project drafts remain visible but are not applied to the current target. Direct-path-blocked starts are disabled visually, for pointer activation, and for keyboard traversal while recovery actions stay available.

Fit Guide wrong-project recovery: Creation-choice and direct Web conversation handoff recovery expose finish-fit-review with the source project preserved across `workdir` and `alignment_workdir` URL contexts when the draft came from another workdir. When creation-choice has no explicit target, enabled Web start links also route to the draft source project, and clearing that draft restores those links to the unscoped creation path before any later Web start.

Fit Guide same-Agent setup handoff target: Creation-choice same-Agent setup handoff routes to the source project so users do not land in a second wrong-project recovery state before copying an Agent brief. Drafts without a source project never merge into an explicit target; Web may start a clean conversation for that target while the unrelated draft remains visible recovery context, and same-Agent setup keeps it unmerged.

Web conversation session switching: Direct Web conversation target URLs start a fresh compose for that target instead of restoring a stale browser alignment session, while explicit `alignment_session_id` URLs still restore the addressed session.

Web active conversation switch guard: Starting a new Web conversation, opening another history chat, returning to top-level composer start, or switching composer paths from an active or still-restoring explicit alignment session is blocked until the active Agent execution is stopped, so users do not silently detach from live work.

Web same-target compose return: If the user has moved to a same-target peer Web page, that page's top-level Compose start returns to the still-active browser session instead of presenting a fresh composer; this remains true after an in-page target-context change rewrites Compose links.

Fit Guide handoff same-project checks: Browser handoff target checks use the shared frontend same-project comparison rather than raw displayed path strings, so local path aliases such as `/tmp` and `/private/tmp` do not turn one project into a wrong-project recovery state. Same-Agent setup exposes a Fit Guide draft as a paste-ready Agent brief only after the fit-review inputs are complete, the reviewed setup gate is ready, and the target project matches; missing-input, missing-source, or wrong-project drafts remain recovery-only surfaces.
Fit Guide direct-path stops use the same target-aware boundary across creation choice, Web conversation, same-Agent setup, and manual expert/import surfaces, so a stale direct-path decision from another explicit target stays visible for recovery without disabling the current target's Loopora starts or expert actions.

Web Tools readiness keeps configured App-home context visible without changing commands: when non-temporary install, readiness, check, or Web-start copy actions preserve `LOOPORA_HOME`, the readiness summary states that this Web session's App home is being preserved; the disposable temporary Web preview action alone does not trigger that custom-App-home note.
Web Tools copyable local command controls remain reviewable before copy: command previews may be shortened/redacted for local paths, but they must wrap within narrow viewports instead of hiding the command shape behind one-line truncation.

Plain doctor output is a human readiness report. A matching running Web instance is described as running and opened, not as a new start. Structured output preserves the compatible `start_web` action kind while `web.access_mode` and action `operation` distinguish `open_existing`, `start_or_open`, and `recovery_required`.
The local Doctor report always owns the target-scoped readiness command when a project target is usable. Tools uses that command and one Doctor-owned Web action across readiness and adapter diagnostics, so neither surface falls back to another port or adds a second default-port Web button. Public Doctor projection omits local commands and target details. Other recovery fields remain machine-readable.

Web-start workdir recovery has matching human and automation surfaces: plain output names the target project, readable project-directory state, recovery actions, and network-auth note without JSON-style field names, while `loopora serve --json` exposes `serve_workdir_recovery_summary`, structured `next_actions`, and top-level action/readiness projections. Copyable doctor/retry commands preserve the current CLI entry such as `uv run loopora`.

Plain Agent-entry, Agent-runtime, and Loop-command target-project recovery reports use the same readable `note` label and current-entry command preservation for doctor/retry actions, while JSON keeps the stable `summary`, `workdir_state`, and action-kind fields, including `loop_workdir_recovery_summary.next_action_kinds` for direct Loop commands. Agent context-card repair and adapter-check fallback recovery preserve that same current-entry command boundary for diagnostic install/check commands. Adapter install-conflict recovery uses the same current-entry preservation for safe rerun commands after refusing to overwrite user-owned files.

Plain Loop-command spec recovery reports use the same readable `note` label and current-entry command preservation for starter-spec and retry actions. JSON keeps `summary`, `spec_state`, action-kind lists, and `<spec-path>` command templates when no spec path was supplied.

Web compose/import/run-start target-project recovery uses the same current-entry command preservation for copyable readiness commands, so Web surfaces started from a source checkout do not hand users bare `loopora` commands. API recovery payloads expose `web_workdir_recovery_summary` or `web_spec_recovery_summary` with next-action kinds and readiness projections mirroring `next_actions`, so browser-enhanced flows and API clients share the same recovery contract.

Doctor JSON modes are mutually exclusive structured diagnostics surfaces: root `loopora doctor` and `loopora diagnose doctor` return a JSON error payload when `--json` and `--public-json` are requested together instead of mixing automation output with terminal prose. Invalid `--web-port` values are also Loopora validation states on these JSON/public JSON surfaces rather than Typer usage output, so automation receives a stable JSON error payload without stderr prose.

Unreadable App database probes are a doctor readiness state, not a raw storage traceback: JSON/plain next actions keep stable unreadable status plus inspect/reset recovery and product-level summary text instead of sqlite exception details, while public reports still omit local paths and commands.
Future-version App database probes are fail-closed Web-readiness states: older Loopora builds must not start Web against a newer local App database, and recovery tells users to use a matching or newer Loopora version or preview an app-scope reset before retrying Web start. Serve startup, CLI JSON recovery, and public doctor next actions project that matching-version recovery with action-kind/readiness fields instead of reducing it to reset-only guidance.

Future-version App database rejection is also a repository write boundary: any surface that opens the local App repository for writes, including Agent Native plan/run/next/submit, direct `loopora loops create` / `loopora run`, and JSON artifact resource mutations such as role/workflow creation or Plan File import, must reject the database before schema DDL or WAL setup can mutate it.
CLI JSON projects this as structured `loop_recovery=use_matching_loopora_version_or_reset`, `status=blocked_by_app_state`, `app_state_status=future_version`, matching/reset next actions, and readiness projections rather than as a traceback, generic command error, or terminal diagnostic line.

`loopora doctor` remains read-only even when the target project directory is missing, not a directory, or cannot be inspected, including path-normalization failures before filesystem probing: it reports that workdir state, leads missing-directory recovery with a shell-safe create-directory command, and otherwise asks the user to choose a readable project directory before install guidance.

Doctor readiness confirmation remains behind that project-directory prerequisite. Local next-step prose says to re-run doctor after the project directory is created or chosen instead of implying readiness can already be confirmed; non-directory or uninspectable targets do not receive target-bound readiness/setup commands, except the read-only support/report command, and plain doctor output defers App/Web reset or start guidance until the project directory is usable.

Doctor public JSON redacts the local path and command while preserving coarse create-project-directory or choose-project-directory actions. Redacted action summaries also describe readiness confirmation as a follow-up after the project directory becomes usable rather than as a return-to-Agent gate.

Target-directory recovery order: Agent entry install/check/uninstall, direct Loop create/run, Web Loop creation, and Web Plan File import/replace recover by repairing the target directory, retrying the requested entry/compose operation, then confirming readiness. Agent Native runtime commands, Alignment workdir-context/session creation, and saved Loop run-start/rerun surfaces remain readiness-gated before retry.

Shared target-directory prerequisite: Agent entry, Agent Native runtime commands, direct Loop create/run, service Loop creation, and Web compose entry surfaces share the same target-directory prerequisite instead of failing at CLI parameter parsing or surfacing raw service errors.

Target-directory blocked surface coverage: Agent entry/setup surfaces (`loopora init <agent>`, `loopora init <agent> --check`, `loopora agent <agent> check`, `loopora uninstall <agent>`), Agent runtime surfaces (`loopora agent <agent> plan/run/next/submit`), and Agent candidate/binding project-root resolution accept an unspecified, missing, non-directory, or uninspectable target path, including path-normalization failures before filesystem probing, only long enough to report stable workdir-blocked meaning.

Target-directory compose surface coverage: direct `loopora run` / `loopora loops create`, service `create_loop`, Alignment service/Web workdir-context/session creation, Web Tools install/uninstall API, Web manual Loop creation, and Web Plan File import/replace use the same workdir-blocked boundary. Where the surface supports recovery actions, it offers create-or-choose-directory, readiness confirmation, and retry.

Target-directory recovery output shape: Plain Agent entry, Agent runtime, and direct Loop CLI recovery name the project-directory state and next actions in user terms while JSON keeps structured `workdir_state`, `next_actions`, and summary action-kind/readiness projections for automation. Direct CLI create/run `--json` projects the same blocked meaning as structured `loop_recovery`, status, state, and `next_actions` rather than requiring automation to parse terminal prose. Nested recovery state must not expose OS exception text.

Target-directory fail-closed boundary: Unspecified, blank, or relative target paths must not fall back to the current directory or receive create-directory commands. Target-blocked entry surfaces must not create the directory, install, uninstall, overwrite, read result files, start runs, claim steps, submit results, create alignment sessions, create Loops, import Plan Files, or inspect managed files until the target is an existing project directory.

Direct/manual/API/service Loop spec-file prerequisites start only after the target project is usable: direct `loopora run` / `loopora loops create`, service `create_loop`, Web manual Loop creation, and Web JSON Loop creation accept an unspecified, missing, non-file, or uninspectable spec path only long enough to report stable spec-blocked meaning. Recovery offers starter-spec creation or existing-spec selection, uses `<spec-path>` templates when the path is absent, and retries the original compose action without OS exception text.

Direct CLI spec/workdir normalization stays inside the shared path-state boundary. Direct CLI create/run normalize usable explicit spec/workdir paths through the shared path-state boundary, including home-directory expansion, before handing them to Loop creation. Unspecified/blank spec paths must not fall back to the current directory.

Spec recovery commands stay representable and faithful to the selected Strategy Source. Copyable retry commands appear only when the preceding recovery action can make the same spec path usable, such as creating a missing starter spec.
Missing-spec recovery orders starter-spec creation, retrying the original compose action, then optional choose-spec; starter-spec commands preserve the effective Strategy Source, including the default `quality_gate` preset or an explicitly recoverable saved orchestration, preset, or readable strategy file.
If the selected Strategy Source file is unavailable or invalid, recovery surfaces strategy-file repair and withholds starter-spec/retry commands that would point back to the unusable path.
Loop workdir recovery also surfaces an unavailable selected Strategy Source file in the same payload, so users do not create or choose a project directory only to discover a known Strategy Source blocker on the next retry.
Direct Loop compose also prevalidates a selected Strategy Source file before App-state or service access, so a broken explicit workflow file is reported as strategy-source recovery rather than an unrelated development reset.

Direct CLI create/run JSON and help surfaces keep the expert path separate from first-use guidance. Direct CLI create/run `--json` exposes the same spec-blocked recovery payload for automation, and successful direct CLI create/run `--json` returns the created Loop plus the optional started run instead of plain text.

The direct `loopora run` help surface is an expert-path guardrail rather than a first-use recommendation: it routes first use out to the read-only `loopora start` chooser, keeps `loopora fit` only when fit is uncertain, preserves separate Fit Guide/Web choices and same-Agent setup branches, keeps review-before-run routing visible, states the existing reviewed spec and target workdir prerequisites, and labels `--json` / `--background` as automation and queued-run controls.

Web JSON Loop creation treats explicit `strategy_preset` / `workflow_preset` as the Strategy Source when no saved orchestration or explicit strategy JSON is supplied, so spec recovery and the created Loop cannot drift to different workflows.

When Loop creation receives an explicit Strategy Source through strategy JSON, workflow JSON, or a strategy file together with an `orchestration_id`, the explicit Strategy Source owns the persisted orchestration identity: preset sources derive their built-in orchestration id, while custom sources keep orchestration id/name empty instead of displaying an ignored saved orchestration. A saved orchestration id remains the identity only when its saved workflow is the selected Strategy Source; prompt file overrides in that mode replace matching saved prompt bodies without replacing the saved strategy.

Missing-spec recovery commands use the same effective Strategy Source priority, omit ignored `orchestration_id` / preset selectors from copyable commands, and withhold starter-spec commands for inline custom strategy JSON that cannot be represented as a CLI preset, saved orchestration, or strategy file.

Direct/manual/API/service Loop execution-setting validation runs before workdir/spec/service preflight. Direct CLI, service, Bundle/YAML loop settings, Alignment executor settings, and Web manual/API compose options such as executor/provider settings, completion mode, command templates, role-model overrides, strategy preset, iteration interval, max iterations, retry count, and stagnation windows are Loopora validation states before workdir/spec/service preflight.
Web manual/expert execution settings expose real browser form controls and may keep a default executor, but visible model and command hints describe the selected execution tool rather than presenting Codex as the universal default.

Invalid execution settings return a stable error payload on JSON surfaces, a form error in browser surfaces, a bundle validation error, or a service-level `LooporaError` instead of Typer usage output, service path errors, or misleading resource-recovery retry commands.

Alignment session creation may keep its product shortcut that command-only executors become command-mode settings, but the Web conversation mode choice remains a real browser control and still uses the shared execution-setting normalizer and command-template validation. Execution-setting preflight must not create Loops, start runs, or auto-create/overwrite the spec file.

Saved Loop run-start target preflight keeps the same target-directory boundary after compose. Service `start_run`, CLI `loopora loops rerun`, Web Loop-detail, Plan File, Run-detail rerun, and JSON run-start APIs must reject an unspecified, missing, non-directory, or uninspectable saved workdir before creating run artifacts, starting workers, or recreating the target project directory. Blank saved workdirs must not fall back to the current directory.

Saved Loop run-start recovery projection makes that preflight actionable. User-facing entries report `blocked_by_workdir`, create-or-choose-directory, readiness confirmation, and retry-start actions without OS exception text or the saved workdir path in the raw domain error.

CLI background run-start failure projection starts after a Run record exists. Worker-start failures from direct `loopora run --background`, `loopora loops create --start --background`, and `loopora loops rerun --background` preserve the failed Run identity, failed status, stable run-start error, retry-start action, and JSON action/readiness projections instead of returning only an anonymous command failure; the embedded failed Run still carries the ordinary Run result retry-start action/readiness projection.

Run result retry-start command fidelity keeps lifecycle failure distinct from task proof. Public CLI Run result output and JSON result payloads label lifecycle failed runs as retry-start state before task-verdict details, and generated CLI retry-start commands preserve the current Loopora CLI entry and command environment while shell-quoting Loop identifiers, so users and automation do not have to interpret `failed` plus `not_evaluated` as a recoverable infrastructure failure or repair copyable commands by hand.

Saved Loop resource CLI output keeps human defaults and explicit automation surfaces together: `loopora loops list`, `loopora loops stop`, and `loopora loops rerun` remain plain by default but accept `--json` for structured payloads and structured error payloads, while `loopora loops status` and `loopora loops delete` remain structured resource operations for both success and error payloads. List/status lifecycle failure retry actions expose action/readiness projections on the returned item so clients can retry without parsing status labels.

Saved Loop resource selection recovery happens before service access. Missing identifiers on existing-work operations (`status`, `stop`, `rerun`, and `delete`) are recoverable saved-work selection states: plain output and explicit JSON surfaces must point to `loops list`, Web saved-work review, and a retry template instead of Typer usage or missing-argument errors; JSON payloads also expose top-level action/readiness projections so automation does not parse prose or command templates.

Saved Loop help keeps reviewed-work ownership visible. The `loopora loops` group help frames saved Loops as the reviewed-Loop library and operations surface rather than a first-use planning path, and routes new tasks out to the read-only `loopora start` chooser, with `loopora fit` used when fit is uncertain, before separate Fit Guide/Web choices or current-host same-Agent setup paths instead of making Web creation appear doctor-gated.

`loopora loops create` help keeps direct saved-Loop creation behind reviewed-spec and target-workdir prerequisites, labels `--start` / `--background` run-start behavior, and routes first use back through `start`, `fit`, Web, or same-Agent setup before `/loopora-plan` and `/loopora-run`. `loopora loops list` help names the existing-work scan surface and points evidence/verdict/residual-risk inspection to `status`; `loopora loops status` help names the structured inspection surface before continuing existing work and states that it does not start, stop, delete, or rewrite work.

Stop/rerun/delete help keeps lifecycle stop, new-run creation, and local Loopora artifact deletion separate from task proof, Loop definition rewrites, same-Agent resume, run-history replacement, target project deletion, source spec deletion, exported Plan File deletion, or external provider history.

Saved Run evidence portability is a private review boundary. `loopora loops export-run <run-id>` and the Run-detail evidence download produce the same curated ZIP projection of available summary, frozen contract, iteration summary, evidence ledger, coverage, manifest, and task verdict.
The projection replaces local absolute paths, preserves useful relative artifact references, rejects curated source files that escape through symbolic links, and stays within a bounded package size. It excludes workspace files, prompts, role requests, raw or normalized model output, provider transcripts, runtime events, and logs by default.
The package declares that it contains private task material and is not a public-safe support bundle, project backup, full transcript, or independent task proof. CLI output is atomic and does not replace an existing target unless the user explicitly supplies `--force`.

Plan File group help follows the same reviewed-artifact boundary: new tasks leave the group through the read-only `loopora start` chooser, with `loopora fit` used when fit is uncertain, before the Fit Guide/Web choices path or the same-Agent setup/readiness path that precedes `/loopora-plan`, while `bundles import/export/derive` move already reviewed plans.

Saved Loop resource retry-start projection makes lifecycle failures actionable. `loopora loops list`, `loopora loops status`, and `loopora loops rerun --json` project lifecycle-failed latest runs as retry-start state in plain/list output and structured JSON instead of leaving users or automation to infer recovery from `failed` plus a low-level error field. Rerun `--json` uses the same saved-workdir recovery payload and preserves background/json retry intent instead of asking automation to parse terminal prose.

Resource CLI list defaults follow the same human/automation split across advanced assets and Plan Files: `loopora roles list`, `loopora orchestrations list`, and `loopora bundles list` stay concise and human-readable by default, while `--json` returns status, count, and the full listed resource array or a structured error payload so automation does not scrape terminal columns. Empty imported Plan File lists report an explicit human empty state rather than silent success.
Human list rows and safe-delete follow-up action projection for these resource groups are owned by `cli_resource_projection.py` so command registration, service dispatch, and resource projection do not drift separately.

Resource CLI selection recovery and file-input recovery happen before service access. Operations that require a selected target/source ID or readable Plan/spec/prompt/strategy file return structured recovery with matching list, Web/catalog, repair, and retry actions on JSON resource surfaces; those payloads expose next-action kinds and readiness projections, while Plan File export/derive artifact-stream operations print human recovery through `cli_bundle_file_recovery.py` instead of Typer usage, missing-argument errors, or artifact-shaped partial output.
Resource CLI create-name recovery also happens before service access. `loopora roles create` and `loopora orchestrations create` treat missing or blank `--name` as reusable-asset naming recovery with retry/list/Web/start/fit actions and readiness projections instead of Typer missing-option output or accidental service mutation.

Reusable role/workflow asset help stays advanced by default. The `loopora roles` and `loopora orchestrations` help surfaces plus Web role/orchestration catalogs frame those resources as advanced reusable role/workflow assets after the default Builder -> optional Inspector/Guide -> GateKeeper path has proven insufficient or reusable.
They route new tasks through `loopora start`, with `loopora fit` used when fit is uncertain, or the Web creation-choice path before same-Agent setup/readiness and `/loopora-plan`; create/derive/update/delete help and Web catalog empty states must keep reusable-asset mutation separate from first-use review, existing Loop definitions, running work, built-in assets, and run history.

Plan File resource help keeps reviewed-artifact ownership visible.
The `loopora bundles` group help frames Plan Files as reviewed Loop artifacts for reuse, import/export, and collaboration handoff.
`loopora bundles import` help and missing-file-input recovery keep import behind an existing reviewed plan file, state that materialized run-ready assets do not create or review a new task, and route first use back through `loopora start`, `loopora fit`, Fit Guide/Web choices, or same-Agent setup/readiness before `/loopora-plan`.
`loopora bundles export` / `loopora bundles derive` help keep the YAML artifact stream and unchanged Loop/run state boundaries visible.
`loopora bundles delete` help keeps imported-asset cleanup separate from the original exported file, source Loop, source project, and run history.

Plan File resource output and validation stay artifact-safe.
Adjacent JSON resource operations remain structured for both success and error payloads, while bundle export/derive stdout without `--output` stays the YAML artifact stream rather than a resource-list projection.
Delete recovery templates lead to `--dry-run` rather than immediate deletion.
`loopora bundles import` missing, invalid, directory-shaped, or unreadable source-file inputs are stable bundle-file validation/recovery states owned by `cli_bundle_file_recovery.py` before service/App-state access rather than Typer path validation failures or App reset recovery.
Plan File export/derive `--output` targets that are already directories, cannot be inspected, or fail during explicit file write are output-target recovery states owned by `cli_bundle_file_recovery.py` with choose-file, artifact-stream, and list-resource actions, so terminal output does not leak local source paths or create artifact-shaped partial output.
Those recovery commands preserve the current Loopora CLI entry and App-home command context like the adjacent structured resource recoveries.

Active-run conflicts are lifecycle conflicts rather than path diagnostics: service `start_run`, repository run registration, Web JSON start, and Agent Native recovery preserve the stable active-workdir-conflict meaning and copyable continuation/stop actions without exposing the local workdir path in the conflict error.
Agent Native continuation, stop, and relative Web-start recovery commands preserve the current CLI entry and command environment, so source-checkout users can copy the recovery path directly.
Workdir identity is resolved rather than string-compared, so legacy relative active-run rows or workdir locks cannot bypass the same-project conflict boundary.

Agent-native saved Loop rerun surfaces keep host-Agent ownership visible: browser form posts redirect back to the Run detail with a same-host-Agent handoff error instead of creating a generic run, while JSON clients still receive `agent_entry_start` so automated callers can continue through `/loopora-run`.

Agent-native candidate semantic lint and repair hints keep the same host-Agent ownership vocabulary as run recovery: new errors describe Agent-native candidates and same-Agent projection, while repair hint parsing remains compatible with legacy `agent-first candidate` text in older artifacts.

Fit-guidance next actions are state-scoped: plain CLI output and structured `next_actions` share the same readiness gate. Incomplete task reviews expose review-completion actions first, while setup, doctor, `/loopora-plan`, and `/loopora-run` actions appear only when no task review is in progress or the supplied review inputs are complete. Read-only support/report actions stay visible for concrete target projects and do not inherit fit-review, direct-path, or setup-readiness blockers, except that placeholder-only target commands remain blocked.

Web Tools target preference scope: Tools target-project browser preferences are scoped to an opaque current local App home/server identity derived from the resolved absolute App home. Reusing the same localhost origin for another Loopora service must not silently restore a stale target project and enable install/uninstall mutations, and the scope marker must not expose the raw server-side App home path.

Web Tools recent target ownership: Tools and standalone Support reuse App recent workdirs as target-project shortcuts rather than creating a second recent-project state. Explicit usable Support target submissions, successful Agent-entry installs, and Web composer session creation remember/promote their target project through that App recent-workdir state; read-only status, workdir-context probes, report-only targets, and diagnostics do not write recents, and later Loop-create/delete refreshes keep Loop workdirs first without erasing non-Loop target shortcuts.

Web Tools target picker mode: In local Web mode, Tools can use the native directory picker to set the target project input; in network mode, native target picking is hidden so users paste a server-side absolute path instead.

Web Tools resolved target propagation: When Tools receives a successful explicit-target status/readiness response, it updates the target input, browser preference, top navigation workdir context, and marked in-page first-use links to the server-resolved absolute workdir so later install/uninstall mutations, public reports, copied commands, fit-review navigation, and peer-surface navigation do not keep using an ambiguous relative path.

Web Tools command source boundary: Copyable Tools install/readiness/reinstall commands come from server structured next-actions or next-commands rather than browser-side executable-command synthesis, so configured App homes and source-checkout `uv --directory <Loopora checkout>` entries survive Web handoff.

Web Tools blank-target mutation gate: When Tools has no explicit target, readiness and adapter status APIs return target-required state instead of the server current directory. Web diagnostics and adapter APIs reject relative target paths instead of resolving them against the service cwd, and install/uninstall mutations, public reports, and copyable install/readiness commands require the input to contain an explicit workdir.

Adapter help/install/check human recovery is readiness-gated: Web start commands may stay copyable, but plain install/check next-step and check-recovery output must present install/verify when needed and `loopora doctor --workdir <project>` before any path choice.
After readiness it surfaces Same-Agent, Fit Guide/Web choices, Plan-file/expert, and existing-work as peer paths, keeps host visibility and `/loopora-plan` on the Same-Agent path, labels Web as a creation/review/evidence surface rather than an observer-only page, and gates `/loopora-run` behind READY preview review in the same Agent session.
Web Tools ready handoff renders one plan copy control followed by READY preview review and `/loopora-run`: a completed fit review replaces the plain `/loopora-plan` control with one message containing both `/loopora-plan` and the reviewed task judgment, while the no-review fallback keeps the plain command and generic examples remain orientation-only.
Structured Agent check JSON must carry the same action path in its summary, so non-interactive hosts do not have to mine `raw.legacy` for the next usable step.

Adapter host-config recovery is fail-closed but repairable: Claude Code settings that are unreadable, invalid JSON, not a JSON object, or incompatible with Loopora's hook merge block install/check as host-config conflicts before obsolete managed-entry cleanup or managed-file writes, preserve the existing file and already-present entries unchanged, and surface `.claude/settings.json` as the repair anchor without absolute settings paths, parser traces, or OS exception text.

Adapter managed-file recovery follows the same public boundary: unreadable managed entry/reference files block status or install as repairable unmanaged conflicts, preserve the existing file unchanged, surface only project-relative repair anchors, and expose the stable `managed file could not be read` status rather than absolute paths, permission details, decode traces, or other OS exception text.

Adapter uninstall cleanup is best-effort and repairable: provably Loopora-managed files that cannot be deleted stay in `kept_files` with project-relative paths and stable reasons such as `remove_failed`, while uninstall output still returns cleanup/reinstall/refresh guidance without OS exception text or absolute file paths.
Bare uninstall help is a recovery router too: it names the supported adapter choice, explicit project workdir, doctor readiness check, reinstall command shape, and host refresh/restart guidance before any files are touched.
Public README first-use docs expose that same safe decommission path: uninstall one project-local entry, rerun doctor, keep Loop records/run artifacts/target files/global Agent config/credentials/external service history untouched, refresh the Agent host if stale slash entries remain, and reinstall through the matching init command when needed.

Doctor plain output must not advertise a blocked Web service as currently startable: when `app_state.web_ready` is false, App state appears before Web start guidance, private recovery archive precedes reset preview, the normal Web command is labeled as after-readiness, and the temporary empty App-home command is exposed as a separate no-mutation preview/troubleshooting action.

Serve help exposes the Web safety boundary before startup: local loopback can be opened directly as the Fit Guide and Web choices entry for users outside an Agent session, Web is labeled as the fit/creation/review/evidence surface, doctor readiness remains the same-Agent/custom-Web-target checkpoint, and network access points users to token auth while keeping `--allow-unsafe-open` framed as an explicit unsafe opt-in.

Development reset preview and doctor App-state warnings only recommend destructive `--yes` when there are planned Loopora-owned deletions. When an App database exists, they expose an exact current-entry and environment-preserving private archive command before reset apply; no-op previews explicitly say no reset apply is needed.

Development reset apply returns users to the readiness checkpoint: after deletion or no-op apply, plain output points back to `loopora doctor --workdir <project>` before opening Web or running `/loopora-plan`, preserving the current Loopora CLI entry and any command environment prefix.

Development reset JSON exposes the same recovery semantics as structured next actions: planned previews review scope, create a private archive when an App database exists, then permit destructive apply and readiness confirmation. No-op previews produce `no_reset_needed`.

Contributor dev commands own their project-directory validation: `loopora dev check` and project-scoped `loopora dev reset --scope all --workdir` reject missing or unusable workdirs as stable Loopora errors before running checks, planning project deletions, or applying `--yes`, including JSON error payloads for automation, instead of Typer path-validation failures or local path diagnostics. App-scoped reset treats `--workdir` as readiness-follow-up context only: missing project directories do not block local App-state recovery, and recovery output asks users to choose an existing project before doctor readiness confirmation.

Shell recovery for Agent slash-command misuse stays route-aware and readiness-first: `/loopora-plan` and `/loopora-run` typed as shell subcommands surface Fit Guide/Web choices for users outside an Agent session before same-Agent setup, then default to `loopora doctor --workdir "$PWD"` as the local readiness gate before users return to the Agent. JSON also exposes ordered `next_actions`, Web route command fields, plus summary action/readiness projections so clients do not parse legacy command fields.
Recovery commands expose adapter-choice install/check entries for Codex, Claude Code, and OpenCode so the user picks the current host instead of silently choosing Codex or falling back to adapter-group help.
When the user supplies an explicit `--workdir`, slash-command recovery, including unsupported Loopora slash names such as `/next`, preserves that target in install/check/readiness/debug commands, reports target-directory state, creates missing project directories before target-bound commands, and withholds target-bound commands for non-directory or uninspectable paths.

Known-workdir execution entry fallback errors preserve the same App/Web recovery as first-use diagnostics: incompatible App state carries exact same-home archive then app-scope reset-preview commands for the target workdir, including configured `LOOPORA_HOME`, instead of placeholders or commands targeting another App database.

Service creation treats local App state opening as a product boundary: if App home, logging, settings, or database initialization cannot access local storage, user-facing CLI/Web startup errors report a stable local-App-state-open failure with doctor/temporary-home recovery direction, while low-level SQLite/OS text, permission details, server-local paths, and diagnostic event names stay out of terminal output. Incompatible schema reset remains the more specific development-reset recovery path.

Web network URL and bind recovery distinguish browser-openable URLs from bind addresses. `serve` startup and doctor/Web diagnostics show wildcard binds as a concrete loopback URL plus a `<server-host>` network placeholder instead of treating `0.0.0.0` / `::` as the primary browser URL. Occupied `serve` ports fail before app initialization or startup-summary printing with a concrete alternate-port suggestion, while non-occupied bind failures expose stable choose-different-host-or-port recovery without OS exception text or local address diagnostics.
`serve --json` exposes `serve_startup_recovery_summary`, `start_blocked_reason`, and top-level action/readiness projections for network-auth, port-conflict, and bind-failure startup blocks so automation does not parse terminal prose.
When App state blocks Web startup, alone or with bind/auth/port blockers, serve recovery leads with private archive then reset preview and marks retry/start commands blocked until App readiness. Plain output renders the same action order instead of presenting alternate port/auth/bind commands as immediately executable.

Web port validation and doctor bind probes are startup boundaries. Invalid `serve --port` values fail before recovery, bind probing, app construction, or startup summary with stable product output. CLI Doctor and Start/Fit recognize a responding same-App-home service (including configured auth) as reusable and ready. Foreign App homes, non-Loopora occupants, and auth mismatches remain port blockers with alternate-port recovery; other bind failures expose bind-target recovery.

Web network auth gate protects non-loopback starts. CLI doctor must not label a non-loopback Web start as directly startable unless a non-blank `LOOPORA_AUTH_TOKEN` is already configured; otherwise it emits an auth-required action plus an explicit unsafe opt-in alternative. User-facing auth-token guidance and copyable recovery commands use a shell-quoted `<token>` placeholder rather than a real token value or a shell-redirection-shaped placeholder. When that token is configured, doctor explains that `web_start` depends on the current environment while still never printing the token value.

Web network auth construction gate is fail-closed before local state opens. Web API doctor treats the current service bind as already running instead of warning on its own port. Non-loopback auth protection is decided from a normalized non-blank token at the CLI boundary before the Web app is built, and `build_app` rejects non-loopback unauthenticated construction before service/settings/DB initialization unless an explicit unsafe opt-in is passed.

Web network host-path actions are copy-safe. In network mode, system API guards reject native picker/reveal calls, create/review surfaces hide native browse controls and show server-side absolute-path guidance, and Run detail trace-material, Plan File import/managed source, and Tools local-asset actions render copy-only server-side paths instead of prompting users to open folders on the browser machine. Native system picker/reveal failures return stable browser-facing `error` / `error_code` values while platform stderr, permission text, and local path details remain log-only diagnostics.
